[Sleight of Hand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sleight+of+Hand) from Amiel brought him [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm), killing [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter), and [Volcanic Hammer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Hammer) for Higure. Max fiddled with his hand in response to the Hammer, representing [Disrupting Shoal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disrupting+Shoal). Next turn, Max brought the other Higure down, and Amiel reset the board with [Wildfire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wildfire).

Amiel had [Izzet Boilerworks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Boilerworks) left, Max only a [Breeding Pool](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Breeding+Pool), and the German did not lay a land the turn after the [Wildfire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wildfire). Amiel had [Steam Vents](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steam+Vents), then [Izzet Boilerworks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Boilerworks), then [Shivan Reef](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Reef), maintaining his mana advantage over Max. The German made [Breeding Pool](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Breeding+Pool) and played out Erayo, with no way to flip it immediately. Amiel wanted to slow Max down even more and tried [Repeal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Repeal) on Erayo.

Max asked the headjudge if Erayo still gets bounced if it flips with [Repeal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Repeal) on the stack. He thought [Repeal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Repeal) would fizzle when the mana cost of the target changes, but since it doesn't change once Erayo flips, he just returned the Soratami Ascendant to his hand. Amiel tried [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm) when Max had replayed Erayo. Max responded with Shoal countering the [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm). Amiel had [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), but Max played another Shoal, pitching [Repeal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Repeal). That didn't counter anything, but it was the fourth spell Max needed to flip Erayo. When the [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm) finally resolved, Erayo's Essence stayed in play.

Amiel played [Eye of Nowhere](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eye+of+Nowhere) into the Essence to be able to play [Magnivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magnivore). But Max had the [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand) for it. Amiel tried the same trick three turns later with [Stone Rain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stone+Rain) and [Magnivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magnivore), but this time Max hard-cast the [Disrupting Shoal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disrupting+Shoal) to counter the [Magnivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magnivore). Amiel was down to one card in hand and restricted to draw-go without even dropping lands. Max had [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter) and Erayo, ready to start the beatdown with [Erayo, Soratami Ascendant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Erayo%2C+Soratami+Ascendant) and ninjitsuing out [Ninja of the Deep Hours](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ninja+of+the+Deep+Hours).

Amiel was down to 13 and his chances dwindled with every card Max drew, both on the turn and from Ninja of Deep Hours. "Very nice, very, very nice", said Amiel, as he scooped up his cards. "After the [Wildfire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wildfire), I drew five lands in a row", and Max admitted that he got lucky, especially with topdecking [Disrupting Shoal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Disrupting+Shoal) in this game.

Amiel Tenenbaum 0 - 2 Maximilian Bracht

### Game 3

"Vore is my best matchup", commented Maximilian, as Amiel replaced a [Demolish](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Demolish) and an [Eye of Nowhere](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eye+of+Nowhere) with two [Smash](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smash) to handle Max's [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter)s. Max went on to say that he should have lost two or three games in the swiss because of bad match-ups: "In real life, the match-ups are incredibly player-dependent. And against players like Amiel, I don't trash talk because it doesn't help against them." Talking about the deck, Amiel asked: "What do you do against Solar Flare? You lose, right?" The answer was that yes, Max loses against Solar Flare if they have [Boseiju, Who Shelters All](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boseiju%2C+Who+Shelters+All). If not, Max said he can win.

"Mulligan to five now, please", demanded Amiel. Max declined. Amiel: "How do you want to be on my team if you don't listen to what I say?" However, Bracht, self-proclaimed team leader, had the better start with [Sleight of Hand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sleight+of+Hand) and [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter). Amiel tried a third turn [Stone Rain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stone+Rain) which met [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand). Max then played [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter), [Repeal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Repeal)ed it to draw a card and failed to make the expected Erayo, or anything else for that matter.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7a/87/7a877f3e2814b8bf7538923611e0849bcfd66c078fac83d6dc339a8f8f3dc4b2.jpg)*In the footsteps of his Honolulu success, and in the Pro Tour thongs, Bracht takes home the Champion's trophy!*

Amiel had three lands on the board, and I was able to get a look at his hand: double [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), double [Stone Rain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stone+Rain), double [Magnivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magnivore) and [Sleight of Hand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sleight+of+Hand). One of the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak)s countered an [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter) which Bracht had replaced with a [Ninja of the Deep Hours](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ninja+of+the+Deep+Hours) earlier. Amiels Slight of Hand gave him the choice between [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) and [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm), and he took the land.

Max ninjitsu'd another [Ninja of the Deep Hours](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ninja+of+the+Deep+Hours) off an [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter), and that gave him three cards total a turn. Amiel was at 10 when he dropped a 5/5 [Magnivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magnivore). Unfortunately for the Frenchman, Max was able to flip Erayo the next turn with [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter)-[Repeal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Repeal)-shenanigans. Amiel topdecked [Boseiju, Who Shelters All](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boseiju%2C+Who+Shelters+All), but the second [Magnivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magnivore) was still stranded in his hand. Ninja attack from Max took Amiel to five.

Max thought long after his attack, and that's when headjudge Philip Schulz asked him if he already had drawn the card from the [Ninja of the Deep Hours](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ninja+of+the+Deep+Hours). That earned Schulz an angry demand for an apology from Amiel, because Max might have forgotten the card. If the card would have been [Boomerang](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boomerang), Amiel would have lost right there. Schulz did apologize, but the incident made the match end on a bad note. But end it did anway, when Max made his Ninjas unblockable via Higure, and that dealt the last five damage to make Maximilian Bracht the 2006 German National Ninja Champion!

Amiel Tenenbaum 0 - 3 Maximilian Bracht

  

## Sunday, August 13: 6:16 p.m. - Tying up loose ends: Third place playoff and the Pro Tour

I also have the third-place play-off match to report to you. Jörg Unfried with Zoo won against Tobi Henke with his R/W burn deck in four games. Game 1 saw Jörg stumbling on mana screw, and Tobias' burn was able to easily capitalize on that. In game 2, Tobias' [Howling Mine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Howling+Mine)s turned against him, as Jörg was able to overwhelm Tobias with a seemingly never-ending stream of creatures. Jörg had boarded in [Otherworldly Journey](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Otherworldly+Journey) and [Kami of Ancient Law](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kami+of+Ancient+Law), but taken out just one [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char) and leaving the Flames of the Bloodhand in his sideboard.

Game 3, Tobias starts off with triple [Lava Spike](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lava+Spike), but fizzles and can't deal with Jörg's creature army. Even though Tobias put [Faith's Fetters](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faith%27s+Fetters) on a [Kami of Ancient Law](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kami+of+Ancient+Law) to lay [Ghostly Prison](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ghostly+Prison), Jörg had a second Koala, dispatched the prison and won. Game 4 is a close affair, but Tobi Henke's Flames of the Bloodhand were just not enough to beat a timely, deadly [Giant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721043356/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Growth) from Jörg.

So Jörg Unfried takes the third slot on the 2006 German National team!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/63/52/6352a8d38e3c31c19e2d698c2cc0ac0c6c18e32ad9ad7649170ae81ce74b4c64.jpg)*Next PT season is a good time to concentrate and take a shot at qualifying for the Pro Tour circuit!*

What else is left to report? Oh yes, the [Pro Tour schedule for 2007](https://web.archive.org/web/20170922001423/http://www.wizards.com/default.asp?x=dci/announce/dci20060811a) has been posted, and the reactions have been very mixed. I had the chance to speak to the players at the players party, where Matthias Wigge was very happy to hear about the Two-Headed Giant Pro Tour. David Brucker, on the other hand, was a little irritated: "I don't even know how that works. I'm not impressed." The one thing everybody I talked to was very, very happy about were the two European Pro Tours: Geneva and Valencia. Even though the locations were not perceived as special, but having two PT's in easy range sounded good for everybody.

The formats didn't raise much excitement. Most opinions about 2HG were along the lines of "I don't know this format, how does it work?" The idea to have one "odd" format in the schedule met a positive response, especially Extended was welcome for some players. Two Time Spiral draft Pro Tours also caused confusion, until players realized that Kobe will be played with Time Spiral only, and Geneva will have an additional set, with the third set of the block being relevant and new for the Block Constructed Pro Tour.

## Sunday, August 13: 8:528 p.m. - The Legacy Champs

![](https://i.ormos.uk/15/47/1547bb1d94c73b5cf54eef23f7862d3a77b98593648bf7074c541baa9ee60648.jpg)*Pretty!*

First, let me show you the gem that is the prize for the German Legacy championships. 137 players were competing in eight rounds, finding out who will be the eight players to fight for this:

Yes, that's Foil Meddling Mage, Regrowth, Mishra's Factory and Gemstone Mine, held in a plastic crystal-case with the words "Legacy Champion 2006 GERMANY" embossed on it. Pretty sexy, huh?

The eight players in the Top 8 were:  
 Sascha Thomsen (43land.dec)  
 Emanuel Sutor (Threshold)  
 Mathias Wigge (Affinity)  
 Christian Hangel (Life)  
 Andreas Müller (Goblins)  
 Stefan Günther (Landstill)  
 Manuel Murr (Yin Yang)  
 Pascal Baatz (R/G Beats)

The playoffs took too long to wait for it, because we had to get the coverage wrapped up. You can find the end results on PlanetMTG.de, but here are the Top 8 decklists:

#### Christian Hangel
